A. Both ſurgeon and patient are to be ſeated in 


3 * D r light, when it can be obtained, or when 


the patient is in bed. When this cannot be pro- 


© cured, proper candles are to be uſed. Then the 


vein is to be elevated by produeing an accumula- 
tion of blood in it, by the application of a proper 
bandage, to be applied with ſuch lightneſs as to 
prevent the blood in the veins from returning into 
the heart; but not ſo ſtrait as to obſtruct the cir- 
culation in the artcries.. An inciſion is now to be 
made in an oblique direction, neither longitudinal 
along the vein, nor directly acroſs it. After the
